  • [core] The Pro gate no longer reads the licence file at each request. Every Pro request — each page of a streaming service, every HQPlayer call — re-read the file and re-checked its signature. The verdict is now kept 30 seconds and forgotten at once when a licence is activated, imported or removed; a refusal still reads the status itself, so a box that has just become Pro is never refused.
  • [ui] The interface reads the licence again when the box comes back, and when the box refuses something for want of Pro. It read it once, when it opened: a reading that failed then — the box restarting — left a box with Pro without its Pro sources for the whole session, and a trial that ended while the app stayed open showed no padlock. It now reads it again whenever the connection to the box comes back — the app back from the background, the box restarted — and at each refusal for want of Pro.
  • [ui] A Starter box is no longer told to buy a licence "to continue". Its licence panel said Purchase a lifetime license to continue, as if the box stopped with the trial — it carries on in the Starter edition, for free. It now says A lifetime license brings the Pro features back.
  • [manual] The manual says what comes with Pro wherever it describes it. Library & streaming never mentioned Pro: the media servers, Roon, the streaming services and the albums Search finds in them now say they come with Pro, as do HQPlayer Embedded, the quick start and the introduction's overview. Troubleshooting's streaming entry opens with the end of the trial and the message the player shows, and the FAQ says what happens to the queue — and that what comes with Pro is using Roon and HQPlayer from the app, not installing or starting them.
  • [manual] Troubleshooting names the message a Starter box shows when HQPlayer Embedded holds the DAC. The list of messages under No sound / wrong output did not have it: the box is on Starter, HQPlayer comes with Pro, and the entry says to stop HQPlayer Embedded from Services or to activate a licence.
  • [manual] The box receives AirPlay; it does not send to it. The introduction, the quick start and Listening listed AirPlay among the places music can be sent to: an iPhone, an iPad or a Mac streams to the box.
  • [site] The landing promises Starter only what Starter does. The unified transport, internet radio and UPnP control point cards are Starter, with Roon and HQPlayer marked Pro in the cards and in the FAQ. The Auto-discovery card named AirPlay receivers, MPD instances and Roon Bridges, none of which the box looks for; it now names what the box finds — the UPnP media servers, HQPlayer, the Roon Core and its zones.
  • [core] The box reads MPD's long answers to their end. It asked MPD to close the connection along with each question, and MPD hung up before it had sent a long answer: some 16 KiB came back. Library → Queue showed 282 tracks of a queue of 1,000; on a library of 3,000 albums the album list showed 245 of them, an artist sent to a network speaker 38 of its 96 tracks, and a search lost its last results. The box now reads each answer up to MPD's final OK, then closes the connection itself. Measured on MPD 0.24.5.
  • [core] Building the album list no longer holds MPD. The box sent all of the album list's questions at once, in lists of 50, and MPD answers a list to its end before anyone else: on a library of 3,000 albums, the box's other questions to MPD went unanswered for 5 seconds, and with 6,000 albums the album list failed. The lists now go one after the other — MPD answers in between (181 ms at worst with 3,000 albums, 23 ms with 474), the list takes the same time, and 6,000 albums are listed in 9.8 seconds. Measured on MPD 0.24.5, x86.

[0.9.72] - 2026-10-15

Added

  • [site][manual] Every page of the online manual has the light / dark toggle. The manual's pages on audiogravity.app followed the theme chosen on the landing, else the system's, and offered no way to change it: a reader who arrived on a chapter from a search was left with their system's. Each page's top bar now ends with the landing's toggle — the moon for dark, the sun back to light — and one choice holds across the site, switched on the landing or in the manual. Without a choice, a page still follows the system. The landing and the manual share one small script for it; in a private window it still switches, for as long as the page is open. On a 320 px phone the bar's gaps narrow so that User manual stays on one line. Tried in Chromium from 320 to 1440 px; not in Safari.

Changed

  • [ui] Every badge you can press has the small button size, wherever it sits. CONFIG (Pipeline), NEW USER (Admin), an Audio Software tile's password and Restart required pills, and a Services tile's ENABLED / DISABLED were 18 to 20 px high in 10 px type, beside 24 px buttons in 11 px — a list of places held the others to the size, and missed them. One rule now sizes every pressable badge, a button or a clickable tag. An Audio Software tile's footer wraps on a phone instead of running past the card, which it already did by 36 px at 320 px. The password pill reads Set pwd; a screen reader hears "Set pwd: the web interface password", the words shown first, so a voice command naming them finds it.
  • [ui] CRITICAL sits at the right edge of a Services tile, under the state it qualifies (UP, IDLE…), apart from the buttons. On a narrow phone, or a computer showing three columns of tiles, it takes its own line, at the right.
  • [manual] The HQPlayer Embedded section names the card's Set pwd.

Fixed

  • [ui] One device can no longer freeze the interface for all the others, and a refused certificate shows in the journal. On a box installed in HTTPS — the installer's default — the interface's server negotiated each connection's encryption in the one loop that accepts them all: a device that opened a connection and stayed silent kept every other device waiting for as long as it stayed open, and with no limit if it dropped off the network without closing (measured: still frozen after 60 s). Each connection now negotiates on its own, within 30 seconds, then has 30 more to send its request; past that it is let go, and the journal says so. A device that refuses the box's certificate, or an address typed with http:// instead of https://, now leaves a line with its address and the reason, where it left none. Nothing changes once a request is in: live updates, the terminal and large files are not cut. A box installed in plain HTTP also lets go of a connection that sends nothing for 30 seconds. Measured on x86 and ARM (Python 3.13) with a silent and a vanished client; the refusals read from Python, curl and Chromium — not tried in Safari.
  • [ui] A screen reader hears the main bar as a navigation, not as tabs. The bar declared itself a list of tabs and held more than tabs — the wordmark, the user, Manual and, in the phone's column, the library's Queue, Sources and Radio — so a screen reader counted those among the app's tabs. It is now a navigation named Main navigation: its entries are buttons, the page shown is marked as the current one, and the library's views under Library form a group of their own, Library views. With a keyboard, Tab now reaches every entry and Enter opens it, as on any button; the arrow keys no longer move from page to page. Nothing changes to a finger or a mouse. Checked in Chromium's accessibility tree; not tried with a screen reader.
  • [site][manual] A page no longer meets a stylesheet or a script from before an update. The host lets a browser keep the site's stylesheets and scripts four hours, and its pages not at all: after an update, a visitor of the past four hours got the new page with the old files — the manual's new light / dark button sat against the bar's label in Safari until a forced reload. Every page now asks for each of its stylesheets and scripts with a stamp of the file's content (style.css?v=881223e621): a changed file has a new address, which no cache holds, and Cloudflare fetches it from the source; one unchanged stays cached. The manual's generator stamps its pages, and its workflow now also runs when a stylesheet or a script changes; the landing and the 404 page are stamped by scripts/asset_versions.py, and their tests refuse a stamp out of date. Fonts and images are left as they are.
  • [ui] After signing in again, the app opens the page and tab you were on. A session that ended — expired after its 12 hours, or ended by the box — sent you to sign in, and back to the first tab: the sign-in page read the destination where nothing wrote it, and an expired session erased it before it was read. You now come back to the page and its tab, query included — only ever to a page of the box itself, read by the browser's own address parser; a sign-out forgets it, and a sign-in page that finds you signed in already in another tab takes it too.
  • [ui] An address no longer opens a tab your role cannot see. /#admin opened the Admin tab for a guest — hidden, its calls refused — and coming back after signing in could take an admin turned guest there. Such an address now opens Profiles. The guard meant to move a former admin off the Admin tab called a function that did not exist, and threw; it works.
  • [ui] The audio pipeline diagram's buttons react to a finger. On a tablet or a phone held sideways, zoom, FIT, RESET, LEGEND, MINIMAP and NETWORK showed nothing under the finger, unlike every other button: the diagram draws in a part of the page the stylesheets do not reach. They now shrink and fade while pressed, from the same file as every other button.
  • [ui] A paused station's Live marker is greyed like the buttons beside it. In the full-screen player, a paused station's Live took the secondary text grey, a shade darker than the greyed Repeat, Previous, Next and Shuffle just above it. It now takes theirs — the text colour at 30 % opacity — in every theme, light and dark.
  • [ui] Chart bars stand out from their ground in Slate and Gravity, the faded ones too. On System, Performance and an expanded Services chart, every bar but the latest is faded so the current one stands out. In the light Slate and Gravity themes the faded bars read 1.5 to 2.1:1 on their ground, and the amber (temperature) and green (network) ones stayed under 3:1 even at full strength — the floor for a chart's marks (WCAG 1.4.11). The light palettes take a darker shade of the same colours, and past bars fade to 80 % in light and 70 % in dark instead of 55 %: measured in Chromium, 3.5:1 at the least in light, 3.4:1 in dark. Minimal, already above, is unchanged. The latest bar stands out less; the figure above the chart gives the current value.
  • [ui] The full-screen player's sleep timer keeps its clock while it counts. Once set, the timer replaced its clock with the time left, and a bare 14:52 beside the queue button read as the time of day. The clock now stays, with the time left beside it.
  • [core+ui] Library › Sources lists AirPlay and the UPnP bridge only when they are installed, and shows one switched off as Off. Both inputs were listed on every box, Idle whether their software was missing, stopped or waiting for a stream. An input whose software is not installed is now left out; one installed but stopped reads Off, with an empty ring in place of the dot and its icon and name faded as an unavailable tile is; one running, or starting, still reads Idle. The screen follows the box live, without being reopened: measured on the x86 box, a stop or a start shows within 2 s, an install or a removal from Audio Software in the same second. Installed means systemd has the unit's file — or its template's, for an instance unit — the test the profiles now share.
  • [core+ui] A webradio started from another app is shown as live. Played on the box's MPD from a phone's MPD client or a UPnP app, at an address the radio module does not know, it had Previous and Next greyed but no Live marker: the full-screen player drew a progress bar stuck at 0:00, and Repeat and Shuffle stayed on though they act on nothing there. The interface decided by itself, from the source and the length; it now reads the box's answer — the rule Play already followed to reopen a paused station: a station the radio module knows, or a stream that names itself and has no length. Measured on MPD 0.24.5, an unknown webradio names itself about 0.6 s after it starts and a file never does, so a track whose length is not known yet never reads as live. A station AG cast to a network renderer is live whatever length the renderer reports, and the playback bar no longer draws a progress bar for it. For HQPlayer, AirPlay, Roon and a stream another app cast to a renderer, the box says it cannot tell and the interface keeps its own reading.

[0.9.71] - 2026-10-13

Changed

  • [ui] In the Minimal theme, what you type into or press has an outline you can see. Fields, lists, framed buttons, selectors, filters and switches were drawn by the cards' hairline, #D0D0D0 — 1.45:1 on a panel, where accessibility guidance (WCAG 1.4.11) asks 3:1 for what tells a control apart. They now take a darker grey of their own, at least 3:1 on every ground of the theme: #858585 in light, #707070 in dark. Cards keep their hairline, the sign-in page is unchanged, and so are the Slate and Gravity themes.
  • [ui] In the Minimal theme, colour marks a state, never an action or a figure at rest. Some twenty green or orange marks showed at once on Services alone, where red, orange and green mean something only while they stay rare. START and ACTIVATE, a service's figure at rest, the running share of a health bar, the stripe and CRITICAL tag of a service the chain needs, the stripe of an installed package and the actions written as badges (REFRESH, CHECK UPDATES, NEW USER, CONFIG) now draw in the theme's text colours; UP, ENABLED, failures, warnings and what deletes keep theirs. The Slate and Gravity themes are unchanged.
  • [manual] Twenty-seven figures show the Minimal theme's outlines and colours. Retaken on 2026-10-13 from the live interface, at the same iPhone 13 mini size and with the same music as before: fields and buttons outlined in Minimal's darker grey, and actions and tags drawn in black (START, CONFIG, TEST, REFRESH, CHECK UPDATES) or grey (CRITICAL). They cover Audio Software, Services, Config, the latency and network tests, the guided editor, the network share form, the audio stack's set-up, Settings, a user card, the system actions, the CPU cards, Qobuz browsing, the radio, HQPlayer Embedded's card, install window and output, the five licence figures, the full-screen player, the busy output, a cast to a network renderer, the mixed queue and the signal chain. Two sentences no longer give a colour Minimal does not show: a profile's health bar is no longer described as green while running, nor a critical profile's tile as edged in orange. The Listening chapter says which transport buttons are greyed, and the Administration chapter that a service's charts unfold in bars, two for network and disk.
  • [ui] A service's expanded charts are drawn in bars, in the theme's chart colours. As the System tiles are: one bar per measurement, the newest one full, the highest measurement written above. Writes were drawn in red and egress in orange, as if they were errors; each chart now takes a colour of the theme's chart palette. The figure above each chart is in the text colour — in the chart's colour it read 2.0 to 3.8:1 in the light themes — and shares one line with its label.

Fixed

  • [core+ui] The players grey the buttons that would do nothing, and no longer remove any. On a station played alone, the full-screen player offered Repeat, Previous, Next and Shuffle as if they worked: Next stopped playback, Previous only reconnected the stream, and Repeat or Shuffle set a mode that did nothing there and stayed on for the next queue; the playback bar offered Next too. The box now works out from its player's queue what Previous, Next and seeking would do — whether another entry follows or comes before in play order, repeat and random included, and whether the entry has a length (measured on MPD 0.24.5) — and both players grey what would go nowhere: Previous and Next on a station alone, Next on the last track of a queue, both while the player is stopped, which then refuses them. On a station alone, Repeat and Shuffle are greyed too, unless one is on, so it can still be turned off; with tracks queued behind a station they stay lit, since they act on those. Paused, a change of what can be skipped reaches the screen within seconds instead of waiting for something else to move. No button is removed any more: the playback bar dropped Previous and Next when a source could not use them, and Play slid into their place — on the last track of an album, under the finger that had just pressed Next; for Roon or AirPlay they are greyed as well. On a phone the playback bar still leaves Previous to the full-screen player. With HQPlayer as the output, Previous and Next stay lit.
  • [ui] Systemd's I/O Acc and IP Acc tags are grey while the accounting is off. They were red, the colour of a failure, for an option turned off; they are now grey and still when off, green and beating when on, in every theme.
  • [ui] UPDATE ALL is orange. The button Audio Software shows while updates wait was red, the colour of a failure; it now takes the orange of what waits on you.
  • [ui] Library › Sources' Scan renderers and Scan network are as wide as their label. Both stretched across their card — 327 px on a phone, 860 px on a computer — and a long bar at the compact buttons' 24 px read as lower than the DSP and Disconnect buttons beside it, though it measured the same height and type. They now keep the width of their label, aligned left like the page's other buttons; the renderers a scan finds, listed in the same column, keep the full width.

[0.9.70] - 2026-10-11

Changed

  • [ui] On a touch screen, a button fades under the finger. With hover kept for a mouse, the only sign of a press was a 4% shrink — under 2 px on a 40 px button, hardly seen on an iPhone. While the finger is on it, a button now also fades to 60%, and comes back as the finger lifts; a dimmed button fades in proportion rather than brightening. A busy button does not react. A card that holds buttons of its own — a radio's star — stays still while one of them is pressed, and the Now Playing cover stays still while its album-details bubble is touched. A radio being swiped away stays opaque, so the red Remove behind it no longer shows through. On a computer nothing changes. The audio pipeline diagram's buttons, shown on a tablet or a phone held sideways, still show nothing under the finger.
  • [ui] Five windows' buttons are written as the confirmation's. The User, Systemd override, JSON configuration, operation log and Support report windows kept the older grey buttons — Cancel filled grey, Save outlined green — once their size was aligned. Shown on mock-ups and chosen: Cancel, Close and the tools (Copy, Download, Upload) are outlined, the window's main action is filled — Save, and the support report's Copy, which is made to be pasted into a message — and Cancel Operation is orange, the colour of what interrupts or removes. A disabled or unavailable framed button no longer reacts under the mouse: a disabled Save still flipped to its outline, because a second copy of its rule, loaded later, won over the first; a disabled orange button and, with this change, a disabled Cancel turned black. The JSON window's Upload and Edit, unavailable while its editor loads, read as such again. A disabled button in these windows shows at 30 % opacity, as every framed button does, where the grey ones showed at 50 %.
  • [ui] Every field you type into has one size and one font. Measured in Chromium, the fields came in a dozen looks — 24 to 42 px tall, text at 11 or 12 px, the network share's in a monospace face: the library search, the HIGHRESAUDIO search filters, the radio search and new-station form, the UPnP server address, the Tidal code, the HIGHRESAUDIO sign-in, the HQPlayer address, the network share and library path, a passkey's name, the network test. Shown on mock-ups and chosen: every one is now 29 px tall, its text at 12 px in Inter — the size of its label — with the same padding, border and corners, on a computer and under a finger alike. Lists keep their own look, save the three of the HIGHRESAUDIO search filters, which share the fields' row; the library search keeps its magnifier; a compact button beside a field keeps its 24 px. The web password an install shows in clear stays in a monospace face, to tell an l from a 1. The active field is marked by its border turning to the text colour, as the dialogs' fields already were: the fields that showed a 3 px focus ring no longer do. The sign-in page does not change.
  • [manual] Eleven figures show this release's fields and titles. Retaken on 2026-10-11 from the live interface, at the same iPhone 13 mini size: the guided editor, the audio stack's set-up, the network share form and the network test, with their fields in the shared look; Audio Software without its DOWNLOAD button; the space under the titles of the latency test, of the licence panel — on trial and activated — and of HQPlayer Embedded's configuration; the HQPlayer Embedded install window offering 6.1.1-5; and the sign-in page of 0.9.69, which still showed 0.9.54. The licence panel's Device ID and Order ID stay blurred.

Removed

  • [core+ui] Audio Software no longer offers to download the package catalogue. The DOWNLOAD button in the tab's header saved packages-config.json, the list of audio software the box works out by itself — what is published, from where, for which machine. Nothing could be done with the file: the box rebuilds it on its own (REFRESH), and nothing takes it back in. The button is gone, and with it the core's GET /packages/config/view, which served it to no one else. The support report still lists every package with its state, its version and whether it runs on this machine.

Fixed

  • [core+ui] Play starts the queue again once it has played through. When the box's player had stopped with its queue — the last track over, or stopped by another app — Play did nothing: the box sent it a pause/resume switch, which that player ignores when stopped, and once the box had restarted it answered that no source was active. Play now starts the current track, or the top of the queue; on an empty queue it changes nothing and says so. Measured on the box's MPD 0.24.5. While nothing plays, Play reaches the selected network player, else the box's player when it was the last one used — not when Roon, AirPlay or another source was, nor while HQPlayer is the output.
  • [core+ui] Play and Pause do what the screen shows. A tap sent a switch, which the box turned into a pause or a play from the player's state when it arrived: a state that had moved in between — the queue running out a second earlier, another source taking over — turned a Pause into a Play. A tap now sends Play or Pause, as shown, to every player — the box's, AirPlay, Roon, HQPlayer, a network player; repeated, either changes nothing. The sleep timer sends a Pause the same way: firing as the queue ended, it could have started the whole queue again. A network player between two tracks is paused too, where it was left playing, and HQPlayer is asked its state at that moment rather than from a reading up to 2 s old; out of reach, it says so.
  • [ui] A screen opened while nothing plays shows the player's state at once. The full-screen player opened from the Library waited for the next change to show anything, so its Play went out with no player to reach.
  • [ui] The player's state no longer arrives twice after the box was out of reach. A screen opened while the app was reconnecting opened a second connection, never closed, and every state then came in twice until the page was reloaded.
  • [core] Play on a paused radio station picks up the live broadcast. After a long pause, the station came back at the moment it was paused, then stopped a few seconds later: the box kept the station's connection open without reading it, the station dropped it, and only the seconds already received were left to play. Measured after a 30-minute pause: the box found the connection gone 20 s after Play, and the sound stopped at 60 s; a network renderer did the same after a 2-minute pause. Play now reconnects to the station — after a short pause too, so what was said during the pause is not kept. A paused track, from the library, a media server or a streaming service, still resumes where it was. This covers the box's own output and network renderers; with HQPlayer as the output, a paused station still resumes where it was paused.
  • [ui] On a touch screen, a button no longer stays lit after a tap. A phone or a tablet has no pointer to hover with, so the browser gave the hover look to the last thing tapped and kept it there until something else was tapped: a tile, a filter, an album, a dialog's button stayed in its hover colour after it was pressed, as if still held or chosen. Every hover effect now waits for a mouse or a trackpad; one that shared its rule with another state — the chosen tab, a pressed button — keeps that state on a touch screen. Nothing that hover revealed is lost: the cover's actions in the Library already show by themselves on a touch screen. Measured in Chromium on the stylesheets before and after: on a computer, every style is the same; on an emulated phone, 111 hover styles are gone and nothing else. The offline page's Retry button follows.
  • [ui] Text in the accent colour reads clearly in every theme. Links — the manual's and the update banner's too —, the manual's current chapter, a favourite's star, the source badges of Now Playing and of the mobile pipeline, the high-resolution value of the format strip, the pipeline diagram's volume and source format were written in the accent colour, under the 4.5:1 contrast text needs: 3.6 to 4.47:1 in Slate and Gravity light, 3.5:1 on grey panels in Slate dark, 3.9:1 for the badges on the accent's own tint. Text in the accent now takes a shade of its own — a deeper indigo in the light themes (#4F46E5 for #6366F1), a lighter one in Slate dark (#A5B4FC for #818CF8); Minimal and Gravity dark, already clear, keep theirs. The lowest is now 5.1:1. Fills, outlines and underlines keep the accent as it was; an icon drawn in its label's colour takes the label's shade.
  • [ui] On a phone, Audio Software's title is the size of every other page's. Held upright — a screen 480 px wide or less — it dropped to 14 px, against 18 px for the others, from a rule of its own that put it on a line above its buttons. It keeps its own line, at 18 px. A card title, on any page or panel, can no longer be given a size of its own.
  • [ui] Every card title stands 24 px above what follows it. The space under a title ran from 12 to 30 px depending on the page, measured on every tab: Services, Audio Software, Profiles, Performance and the logs sat at 30 px, Systemd, System and the licence at 12, Config at 28, and the HISTORY and EVENTS panels at 36. It is now 24 px under every page title, panel header and Performance test, on a phone and on a computer; on a phone, the Pipeline's title, alone in its own card, stands 24 px above the chain too.
  • [ui] The User and Systemd override windows draw their fields. Their 19 fields had no style at all and showed the browser's own field: 20 px tall, no padding, an inset border, and a width of the browser's choosing — 192 px for a name in a 336 px column, 37 to 87 px for a number. They now take the shared field, across the full width of the window.
  • [ui] A multi-line field no longer makes an iPhone zoom in. Safari zooms the page into a field declared under 16 px and does not zoom back out. The app kept single-line fields at 16 px under a finger, but a multi-line one lost to any rule giving it a size: the config editor's multi-line settings were declared at 12 px (measured in Chromium with a touch pointer). They now get the 16 px every field does, drawn at the size of their label. On a computer nothing changes.

Security

  • [ui] A confirmation shows the names in it as text. The confirmation dialog read a text message as markup, so a name inserted into one — a passkey's, a network share's label and the box's message about it, a service, a backup file, the CPU governor, the version an update offers — was interpreted, not shown: a tag in it formatted the question, or could carry a script. Every one of these names is typed by the box's owner or comes from the box itself, so none could be used against it. The dialog now shows every text message as text, whatever it holds; the few questions that need bold or a list — removing a passkey or a user, a refused systemd setting, Update All — are built so that the names and versions in them are text too. A text message that holds a tag is reported in the browser console.

[0.9.69] - 2026-10-09

Changed

  • [ui][manual] On a computer, the tabs stay a bar across the top. The Switch button at the end of the bar turned them into a sidebar and back, and the browser kept the choice; it is gone, and a sidebar chosen before turns back into the bar. A phone, and any screen whose short side is 768 px or less — a small tablet, a 1366 × 768 laptop — keeps the sidebar, as before. The layout now follows the screen both ways: a window carried from a small screen to a large one gets the bar back, where it used to keep the sidebar until Switch was pressed; the sidebar comes back as it was left. The manual says which screen gets which.
  • [ui][manual] Manual stands at the right-hand end of the tab bar. On a computer it followed the last tab; it now stands apart, at the far end, as it stands at the foot of the sidebar. When the tabs fill a narrow window it simply follows them.
  • [ui] The name at the head of the sidebar is a little larger. Audiogravity, beside the licence badge and the connection dot, goes from 12 to 14 px — the largest step that keeps the three on one line with the longest licence label, Version expired; at 20 px that label touched the name and pushed the dot out of the sidebar.
  • [ui] Where the tabs are a sidebar, the connection status shows once. Beside the menu button, a dot and Connected repeated the dot the sidebar carries next to the logo — on a phone held sideways, a 1366 × 768 laptop. They now show only when the box stops answering: Connecting... comes back there, the one place left to say it while the sidebar is closed. On a computer's tab bar, which has no other, nothing changes.
  • [ui][manual] Removing a station, a UPnP server or a renderer asks first. A left-swipe past the threshold removed it at once, so a swipe carried a little too far while scrolling was enough to lose one; Remove from My Live Radio, under a station's ⋯, did the same. Both now open a confirmation that names the item and the list it leaves — Remove FIP from Favorites? — with Remove in orange and Cancel selected; cancelled, the row stays where it was. This covers the swipe in My Live Radio and Favorites, on Sources' UPnP servers and on Audio Output's renderers; the queue's swipe still removes a track at once. When a removal would erase a station added by hand — no other list keeps it, and its address goes with it — the question says so, and in that one case the star asks too; anywhere else it still removes in one touch. The manual's Library and Outputs chapters say so.
  • [ui] Held sideways, the sign-in card is two columns. On a phone held sideways — a screen 500 px high or less — the brand, the version, the core and the licence stand on the left and the form on the right, the footer under the brand; the light/dark switch moves to the empty top-left corner. The automatic passkey panel does the same: who and where and the core on the left, the key on the right. Measured in Chromium at 956 × 440, 812 × 375 and 667 × 375, the card fits without scrolling, and it keeps clear of the notch, the Dynamic Island and the home indicator. Upright and on a computer nothing moves.
  • [ui] The passkey key is filled, as the Login button. Black with a white key in a light theme, the reverse in a dark one; it was outlined, and filled only under the pointer. Under a mouse it opens up as Login does; on a touch screen it stays filled.
  • [ui] The app no longer makes an empty frame at every launch. The API reference's window, in the page from the start, held a frame even closed and with nothing to show, so a blank document was made each time the app opened — a probe took it for the app loading twice after a sign-in. The frame now comes with the reference and leaves once the window has faded out; the reference loads afresh at each opening, as it already did.

Fixed

  • [ui] The sign-in page scrolls when its card is taller than the screen. Held sideways, or upright on a small phone with the passkey button, the card was centred on a page that could not scroll: its top and its bottom were out of reach. It now starts at the top and the rest scrolls into view; a card that fits does not move.
  • [ui] On the sign-in page, the licence stands as far under the core status as the lines above. It sat 4 px under CORE · CONNECTED, read as part of that line; it is now 8 px under, in the header and in the passkey panel.
  • [ui] After signing in, the raw tab bar no longer shows before the app. The tabs written in the page appeared on a blank screen while the app loaded — horizontal even on a phone — and could be taken for another screen; they stay hidden until the app draws them.

[0.9.68] - 2026-10-08

Added

  • [manual] The manual says how to activate a licence key, and how to check one. The Licence section of the administration chapter walks through the License activation window's three steps — key, host, activate — and every refusal it can show, at each step; then Verify existing license and what each status means: ACTIVE is a key already activated on this box. Three figures show the second step, a successful activation and a checked key, taken with an example key and a made-up Device ID.
  • [manual] The introduction lists what each edition opens. Sixteen rows, Starter against Pro, under the names of the app's own tabs. They come from what the app locks: the Library, Pipeline, Systemd and Performance tabs, the playback controls and the full-screen player. The short table above them no longer gives a partial list of its own.

Changed

  • [ui] The small buttons share one size: 24 px, 11 px type. The tiles' buttons — Start, Stop, Restart, Install, Edit Config… on Profiles, Services, Audio Software, Systemd and Config — stood 18 px tall at 10 px; the filters 20 px; Audio Software's Refresh, Download and Check updates 20 px; and the compact buttons of the licence, Admin, System and Performance panels 24 px, but with twice the side padding and, in one of their two families, a lighter weight and square corners. They now share one height, padding, type, weight, letter spacing and corners, on a phone and a computer; each keeps its colours. In a Services tile, the Disabled badge beside Stop and Restart takes their height, and so does the network test's service state beside its Start button. The selectors follow, from heights of 18 to 28 px: Library's Browse pills, Search sources and Radio views (with the custom-station form's Cancel and Save), the log's level buttons and the Pipeline diagram's panel, whose buttons also leave Arial for the app's typeface. The small actions follow, from heights of 18 to 26 px: Radio's + Add custom station, the events' Running / Stopped, the Clear of the history panels, the log's refresh and Live, and an account's Enabled / Disabled. The queue's Refresh and Clear, words of 12 px in the line of the track count, are now buttons framed like the source filters below them; a station's Edit station and Remove from My Live Radio, 44 px tall on a phone, take the small size too.
  • [manual] Ten figures show the new button sizes. Retaken on 2026-10-08 from the live interface, at the same iPhone 13 mini size: Audio Software, Services and Config with their tiles' buttons and chosen filter; the Library's Radio, Browse and mixed queue; the HQPlayer Embedded install window and both HQPlayer cards; and the update banner, which still showed 0.9.55.
  • [ui] The Pipeline diagram's panel rules its sections in every theme. The lines between its groups were white at 10 %, which a light theme did not show; they take the theme's border colour.
  • [ui] The chosen filter is filled black, as in the Library. The filters of Profiles, Services and Audio Software, and the queue's source filters, showed the chosen one in a light grey; it is now filled with the text colour, like the chosen category, source or view of the Library's Browse, Search and Radio.
  • [ui] A dialog's buttons share one size, and a confirmation that deletes says so in orange. They stood 18 px tall on a computer in the confirmations and 29 px elsewhere, and 44 px on a phone; they are now 29 px everywhere, in 12 px type of weight 600, with the small buttons' corners. In a confirmation, Cancel is outlined and OK filled. OK turns orange, the colour of every button that deletes, on the thirteen confirmations that delete or remove something or throw away unsaved changes (Clear History, Remove Passkey, Delete License, Delete User, a network share's removal, Systemd's Remove Override and Restore Backup…), and so does the uninstall dialog's Uninstall, like the tile's Uninstall that opens it.
  • [ui] The installed app opens on its icon, its name and a wave. On a phone it opened on a drawn key beating under its name, at least 2.5 s at every launch, after iOS had shown the name alone in an older typeface. It now opens on its icon, Audiogravity and Your audio chain, fully controlled, coming in one after the other, then a wave going out of the icon for as long as the app loads — at least once, 2.1 s from the first motion. On a black ground whatever the theme: the plain ground iOS now shows before it, so one leads into the other without a jump; in a light theme the app comes out of black in the fade. The app's own Animations setting leaves it animated; with the device's Reduce motion on, nothing moves and the icon's light pulses slowly instead.
  • [ui] The opening screen shows once per opening of the app. It played again after a sign-out, before the sign-in form, and after a sign-in, before the app; a launch with no session cut it short, then played it again on the sign-in page. It now shows at launch only — on the sign-in page when there is no session, the same screen as the app's — and not after a sign-out, a sign-in or a reload. In a browser tab it no longer flashes up before the page.
  • [ui][manual] The licence panel's EDITIONS & LICENSE button is gone. It opened a copy of the editions and of the licence agreement kept by hand in the app. The editions are in the manual's introduction, and the licence agreement is linked from the sign-in page and from the footer. LICENSE KEY moves up beside the panel's title, and the manual's two figures of the panel are retaken without the button.
  • [manual] A locked Pro tab is said to open the activation window. On Starter, tapping one takes you to the licence panel with the License activation window open over it; the manual said it opened the panel.

Fixed

  • [ui] A confirmation opens with its default button selected. It was meant to select OK, but looked for a button by a name none of them wore, so Enter did nothing. It now selects OK, Cancel when OK deletes, or the password field when there is one.
  • [site][manual] Every edition is said to receive updates. EDITIONS.md gave the trial and Starter no updates, and the FAQ, the manual's and the landing's, named Pro alone; the landing's Starter card now says it too. The trial, Starter and Pro all receive the updates of their major version, as the licence server offers them; Pro holders keep a preferential price for the next major version.
  • [ui] Leaving the app no longer stops on an error. On a reload or a sign-out, the app closed its live stream with a call the stream does not have, and stopped there. It now closes it as it does when sent to the background, and the stream comes back if the page stays.
  • [ui] The activation window shows its last step done. Once the licence was active, 03 · ACTIVATE stayed black under a green Activation successful: a step is done once a later one is shown, and none comes after the third. All three now turn green.
  • [ui] The activated licence's Device ID stays inside its frame. Sixty-four characters with nowhere to break, it ran past the certificate's edge on a phone; it now wraps.
  • [ui] In the top bar, each name sits on its figure's line, beside its own figure. Wherever the names read inline, from 430 px wide, a name sat 2 px above its figure and 10 px from it — the uptime's 4 px — and on a phone or a tablet a figure stood nearly as close to the next name as to its own. Each name now shares its figure's line, 8 px from it, and two pairs stand 16 px apart. A figure turning orange or red still moves none of its neighbours. Under 430 px, where each name sits over its figure, nothing changes.

[0.9.66] - 2026-10-06

Added

  • [ui][manual] The app can follow the device's light or dark mode. Settings → Appearance offers Automatic, Light and Dark in place of the light/dark switch. Automatic, the default, follows the device — light by day, dark at night on a device that switches by itself — and so does the sign-in screen, from its first paint. A device set to light or dark with the old switch keeps that choice.
  • [ui][manual] A live radio shows LIVE instead of a progress bar. A live stream has no end and no position: the bar gives way to a LIVE marker under the transport, which dims while the stream is paused.
  • [ui][manual] On a computer, the Pipeline page lists the streams beside the diagram. What plays, through what, to which output — the list a phone shows — sits in the right-hand column, with Audio events under it; on a narrower window, the list and the events sit side by side under the diagram.
  • [ui][manual] The tab menu opens the library's views directly. Under Library, the ☰ menu carries the library's own tabs — Browse, Search, Queue, Sources and Radio, three for the radio, which has no albums to browse — in a tray set in from the edge, smaller than the entry they belong to. A tap opens that view, from any tab, and closes the menu; the view shown is marked by an orange tint and bar, and told to a screen reader. Only where the tabs are a column: the horizontal bar of a computer keeps the library's tabs under the Library tab.

Changed

  • [ui][manual] On a phone, the mini-player gives the title its room. Title and artist shared one line, in the width four buttons left them — 115 px on an iPhone mini — and had to scroll to be read. Previous leaves the bar (the full player keeps it; volume stays), and title and artist take a line each: 159 px of text.
  • [ui][manual] A radio card's actions open from ⋯. In My Live Radio every card carried a green tick that meant Remove from My Live Radio: touching it removed the station. A ⋯ button now opens Edit station and Remove from My Live Radio.
  • [ui] The settings panel and the tab menu are opaque. At 40 % with a blur, the page showed through them: coloured smudges and stray words between the switches and under the tab names.
  • [ui] On a phone, every page's title sits at the same distance from the tab menu. Six tabs left 2 px between the menu and the title, three left 6 px; all now leave 6 px.
  • [ui] Text faded at rest reads again. The cards of stopped services, the dates of configuration files and the PERSIST label were faded down to 2.8–3.5:1, and the number of backups on a Config card and the card of a package that is not installed — one INSTALL away — under the floor too; they now keep the contrast of the text around them.
  • [ui] Check boxes and radio buttons take the theme's colours. They took the browser's blue, and in the dark palette an unchecked box was a white square: they now take the text's colour, drawn in the light or dark palette in force.
  • [ui] System figures are written as they are. Values were set in capitals — X86_64, and MHZ, which is no unit; they now read x86_64 and MHz, the unit smaller on the number's line.
  • [ui][manual] Plainer words in Admin. The live feed's tile is Live updates, no longer SSE Stream, and the cockpit at the bottom of the tab is the UI performance cockpit.
  • [ui] On a computer, the library's lists are centred. The queue, sources, outputs and radio lists stretched across the whole screen; they now keep to 900 px, centred, under a tab bar that stays in place from one view to the next.
  • [ui] The full-screen player uses a landscape screen's width. From 900 px wide in landscape, the cover sits on the left and the track, its controls and what comes next on the right, instead of one column under the cover.
  • [site][ui] The editions speak of a simulation, not a dry-run. The Starter edition's software line, on the landing and in the app.
  • [ui][manual] The top bar names its figures. CPU, Temp and RAM were hidden under 1,025 px, which left three numbers no one could name. They now read inline — CPU: 12% — wherever there is room, and over their figure on a phone under 430 px. A figure takes a colour only past a threshold, orange then red, where all three were always green. A phone keeps those three and leaves the uptime to a computer's bar; on a small tablet, the word Connected gives way to the figures, as on a phone.
  • [ui][manual] Audio Software simulates, and offers UPDATE only when it does something. The DRY-RUN switch is now SIMULATE. REFRESH and DOWNLOAD carry their word next to their icon. UPDATE shows when another version is offered — or for Roon, whose installer always fetches the current build — where it showed on every installed package and answered Already Up-to-Date.
  • [core] The audio pipeline goes out when it changes. It went out every 30 seconds to every screen, identical but for its time. A screen that connects gets the last one at once — to it alone, without the box computing it again — and the pipeline's time is now written in universal time.
  • [ui] The phone's pipeline list stops asking for its outputs every 15 seconds. It reads them again when an output changes.
  • [ui] In landscape, the full-screen player's column is centred on the cover whatever it shows, and a phone held sideways has less to scroll.
  • [ui] CRITICAL reads as a tag, not as a button. On the Config, Services and Systemd cards, the badge that marks a service the audio chain depends on was outlined like the buttons beside it — next to EDIT CONFIG it read as a second one. It is now a tint without a frame, like STOPPED at the top of the same card, and reads CRITICAL on all three.
  • [ui] Every button that deletes is orange. Deleting the licence, removing a station from My Live Radio, confirming a playlist's deletion and removing a passkey were red, beside the orange of deleting a user, uninstalling a package or removing an override. All are orange now, their label too: on a light page the orange border alone read at 2.1:1. The controls that take a colour only under the pointer — a passkey's cross in the settings, a network share's, Clear in a test's history — turn orange as well, and a disabled button no longer fills on hover.
  • [ui] A frame means a badge can be pressed. Every badge that is not a button is a tint without a frame, wherever it sits: FAILED and NOT INSTALLED on the Services cards, UNAVAILABLE and N failed on the profiles, Not configured, Not Supported and Test Package on Audio Software, which sat among buttons outlined like them; and with them the real-time monitor's SCHED_RR and STOPPED, the network test's service state beside START, the counts and results of a validation, ACTIVE and READY on the library's output cards, the performance monitor's and the test history's tags, THROTTLED, the mini-player's source and output, the transport of the Pipeline list. A badge that is pressed keeps its frame, and Restart required, the one button drawn without one, has it back. A tag with no colour of its own takes a translucent grey, which also shows on the grey cards of the guided configuration.
  • [ui] A page title's badges look like what they are. In the titles of System, Pipeline, Admin and Audio Software, every badge was painted the same blue: LIVE, which is not pressed, looked like NEW USER or REFRESH beside it, and white on that blue read 3.5:1 in Slate light and 2.5:1 in Gravity light. LIVE is a tint now, and the buttons are outlined in their own colour — orange for CONFIG, NEW USER and REFRESH, grey for DOWNLOAD, green for CHECK UPDATES, red for UPDATE ALL.
  • [ui] A pulse no longer fades a badge's words. LIVE, I/O Acc, IP Acc, Update available, Restart required and THROTTLED pulsed by turning half transparent, words included: 2.1 to 4.1:1 at the bottom of the beat. The tint of a tag, or the frame of a button, breathes now, and the words stay; a filled badge does not pulse.
  • [ui] The tiles of software that is not installed fade only what is inert. On Services, Profiles and Systemd the whole tile was faded, and with it the NOT INSTALLED or UNAVAILABLE badge that says why; the name, the state and that badge now read, and the buttons keep the look of a disabled button.
  • [ui] The log's levels read in every theme. A level sat on its own tint over its row's, and the level and the time beside it fell under 4.5:1 in four theme modes. The levels are words in their colour, an error or a warning row is marked by a stripe on its left, and DEBUG, at 1.5 to 3.6:1, takes the secondary grey.
  • [ui] Red text reads on its tint in the light Minimal and Slate themes. On its tint, the red of FAILED read at 4.3:1 in Minimal and 4.1:1 in Slate, under the 4.5:1 floor; the red of those two light themes is a shade darker and reads at 4.6:1, and Slate's light information blue, under the floor on its tint too, follows.
  • [ui] The Pipeline tab opens on the pipeline the page already has. The stream hands every screen the current pipeline as it connects; the tab, loaded at its first opening, missed it and asked the box to compute it again. The page now keeps it from the start.
  • [ui] The library's Library tab is now Sources. It opens the list of sources to play from — local, streaming, Roon, media servers — and read twice in the tab menu, under the Library entry it belongs to. The manual already called it Library → Sources.
  • [ui][manual] Library is the last tab, after Admin. In the menu and in a computer's tab bar alike; for an account that does not see Admin, nothing moves.
  • [ui] Manual sits at the foot of the tab menu, apart from the tabs — just above Switch on a computer whose tabs are a column.

Fixed

  • [ui] The System tab shows the memory in use. It read two fields the core does not send, and showed 0.0 GB / 0.0 GB on every box; it now shows the memory in use and the total, such as 2.8 GB / 3.7 GB.
  • [core][ui] A simulation no longer passes for the real thing. It answered Successfully installed …, its log ended the same way, and a simulation that failed left its card in error. It now answers the steps to install … went through — or a step … failed — nothing was changed, its log ends on that sentence and its window on Simulated: nothing was changed, the card keeps the state it had, the history reads Simulate install …, and a simulated uninstall that would delete the settings says so.
  • [ui] Restart required appears only after an install or an update that went through, on the device that ran it. A simulation, an update the box refused and an operation run from another device lit it too, and a press restarted the service — cutting the music — for nothing.
  • [core] A failure repaired outside Audiogravity stays repaired. After a simulation or a refused operation, a card whose package had been repaired from a terminal went back to ERROR until the core restarted.
  • [ui] Audio events records what the players did. When the diagram's first reading of the pipeline failed, the speakers, the amplifier, the turntable and the phone all "started playback". A line is now written when a player starts, stops or moves to another track — a pause and a resume included.
  • [ui] The Pipeline page no longer goes back in time. A reading that came back late, or the pipeline kept for offline use, could put an older state back on screen.
  • [core][ui] On a box with MPD on several outputs, the output pills and the Config tab name the output it plays on. They named the first output of its configuration while the diagram showed the one playing. Asked of MPD, the answer waits two seconds at most: an MPD that stopped answering held the diagram for good.
  • [ui] The update window's title said Updateing.
  • [ui] An arrow key pressed on Manual or Switch in the tab bar no longer opens the next tab. Only a key pressed on a tab moves to another.
  • [core] An MPD that stops answering no longer holds the screens for good. An MPD that accepts a connection and never answers — a suspended one does exactly that — held the Pipeline page, the choice of an output, the library's indexing indicator, the tracks of the album playing and a cover read from its files. The Pipeline page now waits a few seconds at most, the others ten — a cover, asked twice, twenty.
  • [ui] Started without a network, the Pipeline tab shows the last pipeline kept. It said Pipeline Inactive — No audio sources detected.
  • [core][manual] A radio station shows the format it broadcasts in, not PCM. The box read the format from the stream's address: when the address does not name it — …/stream/733765 — the player showed PCM, read as lossless, for a 128 kbps MP3, and the Pipeline tab's list showed no format at all. Both now show the codec the radio catalogue gives the station, the one the radio list already showed. A web stream whose format no one can tell is no longer called PCM; a CD still is. The manual's live hi-fi readout names the codec.
  • [core] A station played from a search keeps its name, logo and format while it plays. The box remembers the last thousand stations seen and let go of the oldest — the one playing among them, after enough searches.
  • [core] Changing a station's address drops the old stream's format. The radio list and the player kept the codec and bitrate of the previous address.
  • [core][ui][manual] A compressed stream shows no bit depth. MP3, AAC, Ogg and Opus store none: the 24bit shown for an MP3 radio was the resolution MPD decodes at — 16bit for France Inter's AAC. The player's FORMAT now reads Lossy there and the Pipeline tab writes the bitrate in its place, MP3 · 128kbps · 44.1kHz; a lossless file keeps its bit depth. A lossy stream never earns the Hi·Res badge, whatever its rate.
  • [core] Tidal's compressed tiers show AAC, not FLAC. Every Tidal address read FLAC; the box now believes the codec Tidal names for the stream.
  • [core] A station the catalogue lists with a video codec — AAC,H.264 — shows its audio codec, and is known as compressed.
  • [core] The radio search's Hi-Res only takes an Ogg station on its bitrate, like an MP3 or an AAC one. Every Ogg station passed as lossless; in the catalogue they broadcast Vorbis or Opus.
  • [ui] The Pipeline tab's list shows the format the box computes, the one the player shows: a CD reads PCM | 16bit | 44.1kHz there, where nothing showed.
  • [core] Simulating an install introduces itself as Audiogravity. Its check that each download answers went out as a bare Python client, on a connection of its own; it now goes out as the download itself does.

[0.9.65] - 2026-10-04

Added

  • [site][manual] A button takes the reader back to the top. A round button in the bottom-right corner of the landing and of every page of the manual on audiogravity.app, shown once the first screen is behind, with a ring round it that fills with the reading. It scrolls up without writing to the address or the history, so Back still leaves the page; it jumps instead of scrolling for whoever asks for less motion, and moves the keyboard focus to the bar's first link. The ring is drawn by a small script on every browser: drawn by the browser itself, it stayed empty in Safari. The landing's logo already led to the top, but nothing said so, and on a phone the top of the screen is far from the thumb.
  • [ui] The Manual window has the same button back to the top. Its chapters scroll inside the window, out of the website's button's reach. A round button in the chapter's bottom-right corner, shown once a full window of it is behind, with a ring round it that fills with the reading: it brings the chapter back to its top, jumping instead of scrolling for whoever asks for less motion, and moves the keyboard focus to the chapter, so the arrow keys keep scrolling it. It sits inside the chapter: a wheel or a finger that starts on it still scrolls the chapter, and at the end it rests under the last line instead of over it — chapters end 70 px lower to make room. On an iPhone, the end of a chapter now stops above the home indicator (not tried on an iPhone).

Changed

  • [ui] The interface no longer loads anything from the Internet. Chart.js, CodeMirror and xterm.js came from jsDelivr, a public CDN: 14 requests at every cold start, whatever the screen, and the device had to reach the Internet, not only the box. The CodeMirror stylesheets held back the first paint, and the module that starts the app waited for Chart.js: with jsDelivr answering 3 s late, the app ran after 6.2 s instead of 0.4; with jsDelivr not answering at all, after 20 s (measured in Chromium on an installed 0.9.64). And the eleven CodeMirror scripts, run in every page without an integrity check, could read the session. The three libraries now ship with the interface and come from the box — 0.2 to 0.5 s to start, whatever the Internet does (measured) — and each one is loaded by the screen that needs it, when it opens: the Performance tab's charts, the configuration editors, the terminal. The service worker keeps them with the rest of the version. The pages' security policy now lets scripts and styles come from the box alone. The terminal moves from xterm.js 4.19, deprecated under that name, to 6.0.
  • [ui] The configuration editors say when they are not ready. CodeMirror now arrives when an editor opens. Until it is there, the Expert editor says Loading the editor…, its text cannot be edited, and Save answers Editor not ready instead of saving; the topology window keeps Edit and Upload unavailable, and they no longer light up under the pointer. If CodeMirror cannot be loaded, both say so, and send to a reload of the page: the browser keeps a failed load for the life of the page (measured in Chromium), so opening the editor again would not load it. The terminal says the same when xterm.js cannot be loaded, instead of offering RETRY.
  • [ui] The tab strips no longer glide for whoever asks for less motion. When the tab changes without the strip being touched — a swipe across the page, a link — the main tabs on a phone and the library's tabs scroll themselves to show it, and the arrows of the library's filters scroll their row. They glided unless the app's Animations switch was off; they now also jump into place when the device is set to reduce motion (Reduce Motion, on an iPhone or a Mac), as the app's other animations already did. A scroll the page asks to be smooth ignores that setting (measured in Chromium).
  • [site] The landing's Now playing strip shows the player screen. Twelve dancing bars suggested a visualiser the app does not have, two chains told one path two different ways, the chain went from Roon straight to the HQPlayer NAA — leaving out HQPlayer, which makes the DSD256 — and began at iOS, which the sound never leaves. The strip now shows what the app's player screen shows, in the page's style: the album cover, the signal path (Qobuz → HQPlayer → HQPlayer NAA → USB → Heed Abacus), the four format cells (DSD256 · 11.3MHz · 22.6Mbps · DSD), the progress with the time left and the output, and a link to the signal-path diagram below it. The track is Come Together, from Abbey Road, 4:19 on Qobuz.
  • [site] Every text on the landing meets the contrast floor, in both themes. 29 texts read under 4.5:1 in the light theme and 26 in the dark one (measured in Chromium, element opacity counted): green and orange as text on white (2.3 and 2.2:1), the section labels and the palest grey (3.2 to 3.5:1), the footer, the code comments, the ghosted numbers of the five-layer stack (1.7:1), the bar's date. None does now: green and orange take a darker shade as text in the light theme, the palest grey goes one step up, the always-dark blocks keep their own greys. The signal-path diagram's lines, arrows, frames and moving dots, and the carousel's dots, are above the 3:1 a graphic needs.
  • [site] The landing's layout holds together. The backgrounds alternate down the page again: the figures under End the jitter now share its band. The feature cards are ordered by height, which cut their empty space from 1,378 to 820 px at 1440 px. The board-and-software list reads at 75 characters a line, where it ran to 165; the installation list's values start on one line. The signal-path diagram fills its frame, its smallest labels grow from 9 to 11.3 px at 1440 px, it lists HQPlayer Embedded among what runs on the box, and it names the same DAC as the strip, the Heed Abacus. The brand's raised ty no longer pushes its line down, and stays lowercase in capitalised labels, as in the logo.
  • [site] The landing's first screen on a phone shows the product. The first capture of the app came 1,070 px down. The bar now holds one line, the links row fades at its edge to say it scrolls, the install button comes under the title, the capture right after it — its top half on the first screen — then a shorter hook that says nothing the long one does not. The green Public beta over the button is gone; the ribbon stays, and no longer covers the bar's text or the theme toggle under 1100 px.

Fixed

  • [core][ops][ui][manual] An update that does not go through says why, under the update banner. Whatever stopped it, the banner said Update failed — previous version restored, and the reason the updater recorded — reinstall failed for any failure of the installer — reached no screen. It now gives the installer's own words — a system turned down (This system (Debian GNU/Linux 12 (bookworm)) has glibc 2.36; this core needs 2.38 or later…), a download that failed, a damaged package — or says that after the install the box was not running the new version, or that the updater could not be started. The reason stays under the banner and is read back when the Admin page is opened again or from another device, for admins only: they alone can act on it. The manual says the box tells why.
  • [ops] An installer that never arrives no longer restarts the core. When the installer cannot be fetched — no network, or an answer that is not a script, a captive portal's — nothing on the box has changed yet: the update now stops there and says so, without stopping and restarting the core.
  • [ops] The update log keeps about the last twenty updates. Each update added 10–15 KB to it and nothing ever shortened it — 749 KB on a box updated 52 times. It now keeps its last 256 KB, cut before each update, in a way a full disk cannot empty.
  • [ui] The update banner no longer acts once it is off the page. An answer that arrived after the banner was removed still set its state and the Admin tab's badge.
  • [ops] Updating the interface no longer leaves the old versions' files behind. The installer copied each release over the last one and removed nothing, and a release names its files after their content: every update left about 17 files (1.2 MB) for good (measured on the 0.9.62 → 0.9.63 → 0.9.64 packages), and a validation box held 944 files, 94 MB, for a version that ships 25. The installer now keeps two versions — the new one, and the one it replaces, which a page left open during the update still loads from — and removes the rest: the files it recorded for older versions, whatever else lies in the folders the interface ships, and the source maps older versions left. What the box writes for itself — the interface's settings file, its certificates — is never touched. The first update made with this installer records what it installs, and removes the older files at the next one; the source maps go at once. Nothing is removed when what is in place is not what the installer recorded — a copy made by hand, an older installer — nor when the new package cannot be read whole, and two installs at once wait for each other.
  • [ui] A page left open through two updates no longer opens empty screens. The box keeps the files of two versions: a page older than that asked for files that were gone, and the screen it opened stayed empty — without a service worker, nothing reloaded it. When a screen's code is missing, the page now asks the box which version it serves and, if it is a newer one, reloads onto it, once a minute at most. When the box serves the same version — a network blip, a server restarting — nothing is reloaded, and a message says that part of the screen could not be loaded, and to reload the page.
  • [ui] Reconnecting the terminal no longer stacks a second one. RECONNECT and RETRY opened a new terminal under the one still on screen, whose size kept being adjusted for nobody.
  • [ui] Edit, pressed right after the topology window opens again, keeps the editor editable. Pressed within its first moments, it was set back to read-only when the file was shown.
  • [ui] A new version of the interface reaches an open app in seconds, no longer five minutes later. After an update of the box, the app said Updating… A new version is being applied and nothing followed: the service worker relayed the app's two live streams — the player's and the dashboard's — and the version in place, kept busy by them for as long as the app stayed open, could not hand over. Chromium forced it after five minutes, and the page reloaded then, with no word (measured in Chromium 145 without a debugger attached: one changes these timings). The streams now go straight to the box, and the page reloads onto the new version within 2 to 10 s, 12 s when it has to reload itself (below) — 19 attempts out of 19, the app open during the update, opened right after it, or in two tabs. A new version already waiting when the app opens — installed while no page listened — is told too: it waited until every window of the app was closed, a reload included. Told, a new version still stayed waiting in a third of the attempts with the app opened right after an update, with nothing left in its way; a reload let it in every time, so the page now reloads itself 10 s after Updating… when the new version has not come, once in five minutes at most, and tells a version still waiting again at its five-minute check. A player stream that loses the box still spaces its attempts out — 3, 7, 15, then 31 s, as before. Safari not tried. The update to this version still meets, on each device, the service worker of the version before, which relays the streams: it applies when the app is reloaded or closed, or after those five minutes.
  • [site][ui] The third-party notices list what the interface ships. Chart.js and CodeMirror were listed as loaded from a CDN, xterm.js was missing, and Cytoscape was still there.
  • [site] The signal-path diagram's dots no longer wait in its corner. For up to 1.8 s after the page loaded, three of the moving dots sat at the drawing's top-left corner before setting off; they now start already under way.
  • [manual] The manual's links read in the light theme. On audiogravity.app they were the green that reads 2.3:1 on white; they now take the darker green (5.0:1), and the active chapter's mark the green kept for lines (3.3:1).

Removed

  • [ui] Cytoscape and cytoscape-dagre are gone. Nothing in the interface used them any more — the audio chain is drawn in SVG — and they brought lodash, dagre and graphlib into the build.
  • [core][ops] SELF_UPDATE_BOOTSTRAP_URL is gone. The updater fetches the installer from the address it carries, which its root launcher lets no caller change: the key steered nothing, and edited, it would have lied. Updated boxes lose it — the core refuses a key it does not know — and the support report shows the address the installed updater uses.

[0.9.64] - 2026-10-02

Added

  • [ui][manual] A Settings switch hides the top bar's figures, on this device. Top Bar Metrics, under Animations and on by default: switched off, the top bar no longer shows uptime, CPU, temperature and memory, and no longer handles them — they still reach the device, on the stream every live update travels on, and the System tab keeps showing them. Switched back on, it shows at once the last reading of the live stream, or dashes if there is none yet, and asks the box nothing: the box's own answer is partial, and its CPU figure covers the few milliseconds since the stream's last one. Each device keeps its own choice. The manual lists it in the Settings panel, whose figure is retaken.

Changed

  • [site] The landing promises what the app does. Profiles are no longer sold as atomic — a service that fails does not undo the switch — but by their live health bar. The service tiles' sparklines are live, not 60 s, and the throughput reference the tile quoted is dropped: it went with the INFO windows in 0.9.63, and its figures were eight times too high. The configuration editor offers a diff and an automatic backup restored in one click, not live validation. The passkeys card no longer mentions a session history the app does not keep. The login screenshot is retaken.

  • [ui] What acts as a button is a button. The Enabled/Disabled badge of an account, the four actions of the Audio Software title (refresh, download, CHECK UPDATES, UPDATE ALL), the metrics icon of the Services title, the API icon of the Settings panel, the footer's icon, the player's source dots, in the bar and full screen, and the pull tab were spans and divs made to act as buttons, with code of ours for the keyboard. They are real buttons now, and look exactly as before — measured in Chromium, on a phone and a desktop, light and dark: same boxes, same pixels. The browser itself gives them Tab, Enter and Space, and announces them. A test fails on an element announced as a button that is not one, bar the two below.

  • [ui] The CPU cards of the Performance tab draw their load in bars, as the System tiles do. Sixty samples, the time they cover written beside Load, a gap where the stream paused — and one 0–100 % scale for every core: each card used to scale its curve to its own values, so a core idling between 1 and 3 % drew as tall as one at 80 %. CPU n and its socket and core now share one line, and the chart takes the line this frees: nearly twice as tall, on a card of the same height (measured in Chromium at every width the grid gives a card, with and without the THROTTLED badge, which cuts the socket and core short on a narrow card rather than push them onto a second line). A measured zero keeps a 1 px bar, here and on the System tiles, so a core idling at 0 % no longer looks like a moment nothing was measured.

  • [ui] The volume slider sends its value four times a second at most. A range slider fires on every value it crosses, and each one became a request: 32 for a third of the course dragged in a second, 101 for the whole course in two (measured in Chromium) — on HQPlayer, two connections to the player each. It now sends the first value at once, then the latest every quarter of a second, and the value it stops on as soon as the gesture ends: 8, 3 and 13 requests for the same drags, the last one always where the slider stopped. The step buttons and the keyboard still send each press at once, and nothing is sent once a gesture has ended, so a late value cannot reach a source switched to in the meantime. Not measured on an iPhone.

  • [core] HQPlayer's volume is left to HQPlayer. Audiogravity kept it with the DSP settings and sent it back each time the HQPlayer card reconnected: the level last changed through that Audiogravity — two instances watching the same HQPlayer kept −10.2 and −5.4 dB — so a volume lowered in HQPlayer itself was set back at once. It is no longer kept nor sent back, and HQPlayer's settings file is no longer rewritten at every step of the slider. A snapshot of HQPlayer's settings that matches none of its lists is no longer taken again every time the card is shown.

  • [site] The landing's Settings view no longer offers the configuration export. Its caption still read Export the whole configuration to a file, and a note pointed at the export, which left Settings in 0.9.62. The caption now says what the panel holds; the capture itself, from 0.9.45, is still to be retaken. The push notifications card says an audio service where it said a critical service — in the app, critical is a kind of profile, and the alert covers every audio service Audiogravity follows. The HQPlayer card says that each HQPlayer keeps its own filter, shaper and mode.

  • [manual] The manual says what DSD does to the volume, and what Audiogravity writes in the topology. Listening: while a DSD track plays, the volume is set to 100 and the volume control gives way to a padlock, then the volume goes back to where it was — with the app closed too, so the listening level belongs on the amplifier; through HQPlayer or a network speaker, the volume stays adjustable. Outputs & engines: each HQPlayer keeps its own filter, noise shaper and mode. Administration: a value of the wrong kind blocks the topology's save; you can always roll back promised what no screen offers, so the advice is now to Download the file before a big change; and the topology file is no longer said never to be rewritten — the output chosen for each service is written there.

  • [manual] The Performance chapter shows what it describes. It had no figure, and never said what a CPU card holds: it now shows two cards — frequency, temperature and its colours, load in bars, governor — a latency test after a run, and the network test. The Terminal says that closing it, or the end of the session, stops what runs in it, a command started with nohup included.

  • [core] Reading the temperatures no longer holds the core up. It happened in the core's own loop: on the x86 test box, whose Apple chip exposes 60 sensors, each reading stopped the core for up to 56 ms — no request answered, no live update sent — every twenty seconds or so while the app is open. It now happens aside: 6 ms at worst, 1 ms in 99 cases out of 100 (measured on that box). Nothing changes on a Raspberry Pi, whose single sensor took about 1 ms. Callers arriving together share one reading.

  • [core] Playing no longer keeps systemd loading services. While something plays and a screen follows it, the audio chain is rebuilt every second, and each rebuild asked the box's systemd about every service the topology names — a share on the NAS, an app on the phone, Roon Server on another machine — and read the details of every known audio service, running or not. Asked about a unit it does not hold in memory, systemd loads it, then drops it again: 35 loads a second on the x86 test box while it played with two screens open, 5.9 % of a core in systemd and 2.2 % in the system bus (measured). Only the box's own services are asked now, all in one call that makes systemd load none of them, and a service's process is looked up only while it runs: playback causes no load at all, and systemd and the bus take 0.2 % of a core at most each (measured on the same box, playing, one screen open or none). The service-down watch reads its services the same way, and the check that decides at start whether to connect to Roon no longer makes systemd load a unit either.

  • [core] With the app closed, playback no longer rebuilds the audio chain every second. The player's watch read every source and laid out the whole chain — devices, links, the other machines probed over the network — every second of playback, for the DSD protection alone once no screen was open. With nobody following the player, it now reads the sources only while a DSD track plays, while HQPlayer can play on the box (its NAA or HQPlayer Embedded running) or while Roon Bridge or Roon Server runs — the sources that report none of their changes — and never lays the chain out then. MPD reports its own: its DSD is caught the moment it starts. Measured on the x86 test box, radio playing, app closed: the core from 11.8 to 2.2 ms of CPU per second. With a screen open, or a client of GET /sse/player, nothing changes.

  • [core] A sound card without a volume control is no longer asked for one again and again. A DAC whose volume is fixed — an HDMI or S/PDIF output, many USB DACs — has no volume for Audiogravity to read, and it asked again every 5 seconds while the music played: whenever the app is open, and with it closed whenever HQPlayer plays through the box (its NAA or HQPlayer Embedded), Roon Bridge runs or a DSD track plays. Each time, amixer was started up to nine times: 27 in eleven seconds of playback on the x86 test box's HDMI output (measured). The card is now asked once, and again only when its controls change, when it is unplugged or replaced, or when a DSD track starts; the HDMI outputs of the ARM test box, which have no control at all, are asked once too. While DSD plays, the protection that keeps the volume at 100 read the whole card every second: it now reads the volume only — nothing from a card without one, one amixer instead of eight; one instead of two from a DAC already at 100 (measured on both boxes). A card the core cannot open is not asked at all, and a volume read at the very moment it changes is no longer kept for a minute.

  • [core] A Roon Server declared in the topology is probed every 30 seconds, like the other machines. Its address was tried at every rebuild of the audio chain — every second while the music plays with a screen open — and each rebuild waited half a second when the machine did not answer: 510 ms to build the chain instead of 8 on the x86 test box, whose topology names a Roon Server that is not there (measured). Its state is now kept 30 seconds, as those of a NAS, MinimServer or a remote HQPlayer are, and the chain is rebuilt once a second again, as intended. When Roon Bridge on the box is connected to the Core, the Core itself is still asked at every rebuild, so Roon stopping shows at the next one.

  • [core] Keeping the profiles up to date no longer starts programs. Every 30 seconds from the box's start, app open or not, the watch behind the profiles read their services through systemctl: one read of 36 properties where it needs three answers per service, then one more systemctl for each stopped service — asking what the first read had just answered — and one for each service not installed, which started a script of the old init system on top and was asked again every time. Seven programs every 30 seconds on the x86 test box: 110 ms of CPU, 161 ms on the ARM test box (measured). Two questions to systemd over D-Bus answer now, starting nothing and making it load nothing: about 8 ms on the x86 box, 9.5 ms on the ARM box, and systemd's own share on the x86 box drops from 1.3 to 0.3 ms per second (measured, app open). When D-Bus does not answer, a single systemctl with the few properties needed takes over.
